### Branch / HEAD / Dirty State

### What Changed
- I fixed the first real lowering symmetry bug uncovered by the GB200 census:
  - Gluon / layout synthesis already had a canonical `M=64` split-N register
    family (`getCanonicalM64SplitNLayout(...)`)
  - lowering still treated that same family as eligible for the scalar
    `I32x32b` fast path

    runtime-matrix misaligned-address bucket

## 2026-04-10 MMAv5 Root-Plan Producer Fix Checkpoint

### Branch / HEAD / Dirty State
- Branch:
  - `codex/tmem-isolated`
- HEAD at the start of this checkpoint:
  - `0889ded4e`
- Dirty files:
  - `include/triton/Dialect/TritonNvidiaGPU/IR/TensorMemoryUtils.h`
  - `lib/Dialect/TritonGPU/Transforms/AccelerateMatmul.cpp`
  - `lib/Dialect/TritonNvidiaGPU/IR/TensorMemoryUtils.cpp`
  - `lib/Dialect/TritonNvidiaGPU/Transforms/PromoteLHSToTMem.cpp`
  - `python/src/gluon_ir.cc`

### What Changed
- The old live unit/regression bucket turned out to be a producer-side MMAv5
  root-contract bug, not another direct-planning or raw-vs-query-type issue.
- I renamed the helper to `getMMAv5RootRowPlan(...)` and made the contract
  explicit:
  - it now represents the shared `M=64` MMAv5 root TMEM row-plan for mutable
    accumulators and source-initialized operand roots;
  - it is no longer framed as accumulator-only; and
  - it now accepts higher-rank roots when the trailing TMEM row dimension is
    `64`, because the physical contract is still the full `128`-row backing
    tile.
- `PromoteLHSToTMem` now annotates the non-mutable TMEM roots it creates with
  that explicit row plan.
- Existing root-plan annotations in `AccelerateMatmul.cpp` and `gluon_ir.cc`
  were retargeted to the renamed shared helper.

### Why This Was The Core Bug
- The representative failing kernels (`test_dot[...]`, `test_lhs_in_tmem[...]`,
  `test_simple_matmul[...]`, and `test_cast_matmul[...]`) all used
  source-initialized `M=64` TMEM roots.
- Mutable accumulator roots already carried explicit `ttng.tmem_ldst_row_plan`
  metadata and lowered to packed `16x32bx2` stores/loads.
- The source-initialized non-mutable roots created by `PromoteLHSToTMem` did
  not, so they fell back to the wrong logical-`64` interpretation and emitted
  scalar `32x32b.x1`-style root accesses.
- The fix is therefore architectural:
  - MMAv5 root row plans are producer-owned metadata for all `M=64` roots, not
    an accumulator-only special case.

### Validation After `make -j8`
- Passing exact reruns:
  - `python/test/unit/language/test_core.py::test_dot[1-64-64-64-4-False-False-none-tf32x3-float32-float32-1-None]`
  - `python/test/unit/language/test_matmul.py::test_lhs_in_tmem[float32-False-64-128-32]`
  - `python/test/unit/language/test_matmul.py::test_simple_matmul[True-False-4-1-64-512-32-2-float32-tensorfloat32]`
  - `python/test/regression/test_cast_matmul.py::test_cast_matmul[768-768-1024-16-64-16-bfloat16-float16-float16]`
  - `python/test/gluon/test_core.py::test_tmem_descriptor_chain_matrix[linear_m64_32x32b_splitn_8w-layout9-64-128-32x32b_splitn-8-16x32bx2]`
  - `python/test/gluon/test_tmem_runtime_matrix.py::test_tmem_runtime_matrix_splitn_rowcol_permuted_layout_sweep[identity-identity-2-32x32b_splitn]`
  - `python/test/gluon/test_core.py::test_mma_shared_inputs[False-ctas_per_cga0-1-1-1-64-0-32-warps2-8-False-True-acc_dtype0]`
  - `python/test/gluon/test_core.py::test_block_m_64_mma[linear]`
- File-level reruns:
  - `python/test/regression/test_cast_matmul.py`
    - `1080 passed, 216 skipped`
  - `python/test/unit/language/test_matmul.py`
    - `3 failed, 758 passed, 4780 skipped`

### Remaining Exact Current-Head Failures
- Descriptor-view row-anchor materialization bucket:
  - `python/test/unit/language/test_matmul.py::test_simple_persistent_matmul[False-4-64-128-32]`

## 2026-04-10 Direct-Planning Split-N Repair Checkpoint

### What Landed Locally
- `TensorMemoryUtils.cpp` now distinguishes the analyzed descriptor view image
  from the active physical TMEM image for direct `ld/st` planning:
  - when the analyzed view has a zero row basis but the active physical layout
    already matches the logical shape, the direct planning query now runs on
    the active layout instead of the lifted support image;
  - `canInvertAndComposeSafely(...)`, `invertAndCompose(...)`, and
    logical-row-anchor solving all use that active direct-planning layout; and
  - the old row-zero direct-view repair shims stay disabled on that path so
    they do not reintroduce the wrong lifted support-band arithmetic.

### Exact Validation After Rebuild
- `make -j8`
- passing exact reruns:
  - `python/test/gluon/test_core.py::test_tmem_descriptor_chain_matrix[linear_m64_32x32b_splitn_8w-layout9-64-128-32x32b_splitn-8-16x32bx2]`
  - `python/test/gluon/test_core.py::test_tmem_linear_roundtrip_splitn_shapes[linear_m64_splitn_64x32-layout11-64-32-expected_offset_imms11]`
  - `python/test/gluon/test_tmem_runtime_matrix.py::test_tmem_runtime_matrix_splitn_rowcol_permuted_layout_sweep[identity-identity-2-32x32b_splitn]`
  - `python/test/gluon/test_core.py::test_mma_shared_inputs[False-ctas_per_cga0-1-1-1-64-0-32-warps2-8-False-True-acc_dtype0]`
  - `python/test/gluon/test_core.py::test_block_m_64_mma[linear]`
- still failing exact reruns:
  - `python/test/unit/language/test_core.py::test_dot[1-64-64-64-4-False-False-none-tf32x3-float32-float32-1-None]`
    - `CUDA error: misaligned address`
  - `python/test/unit/language/test_matmul.py::test_simple_matmul[True-False-4-1-64-512-32-2-float32-tensorfloat32]`
    - `261757 / 524288` mismatches (`49.9%`)
  - `python/test/unit/language/test_warp_specialization.py::test_warp_specialize_attention_forward[True-4-False-3-64-64-1024-1024]`
    - `CUDA error: misaligned address`

### Important Negative Results
- I tried two follow-up experiments while reducing the checkpoint, and both are
  now backed out:
  - preferring query-type lowering before raw-query for root TMEM allocs in
    `TensorMemoryToLLVM.cpp`; and
  - reordering MMAv5 canonical-family vs analyzed-view address arithmetic in
    `MMAv5.cpp`.
- Neither experiment fixed the remaining unit regressions, and neither is part
  of the current local diff anymore.

### Current Reading
- The direct split-N TMEM ld/st bug was real and is now fixed on exact reruns.
- The remaining representative branch-caused unit failures are narrower and no
  longer look like the same direct-planning bug:
  - they live in the MMAv5 producer-side dot / matmul / warp-specialization
    family.
- The durable GB200 exact manifests in this folder still reflect the
  pre-fix census, so they remain useful for backlog shape but should not be
  treated as current counts until the next broader rerun regenerates them.

### Next Concrete Steps
1. Commit the direct-planning split-N fix by itself.
2. Compare the remaining unit failures against merge-base PTX/TTGIR again from
   the narrowed post-fix baseline.
3. Focus next on MMAv5 producer-side address/family selection while keeping

### PTX / TTGIR Root-Cause Clue
- Representative wrong-code kernel:
  - `python/test/unit/language/test_matmul.py::test_simple_matmul[True-False-4-1-64-512-32-2-float32-tensorfloat32]`
- Forced `16x256` variant:
  - current branch still uses `tcgen05.ld.sync.aligned.16x256b.x16.b32`, but
    the final load offsets are contiguous:
    - `+0`
    - `+128`
    - `+256`
    - `+384`
  - merge-base uses the same family with the second support band lifted:
    - `+0`
    - `+128`
    - `+1048576`
    - `+1048704`
- Non-forced sibling:
  - current branch chooses `16x32bx2.x64`
  - merge-base chooses `32x32b.x64`
- Best current interpretation:
  - the new row-plan-aware canonical-`M=64` family selection / packet
    decomposition is collapsing a support-band / lifted-image bit into ordinary
    packet repetition
  - this fits the TMEM split-N regressions, the branch-added runtime-matrix
    failures, and the broader tf32/tf32x3 matmul wrong-code much better than a
    cache-poisoning or generic LLVM-noise theory

- no classification sweeps are still running

## Fixed Bug
Exact repro that is now green:
- `python/test/gluon/test_core.py::test_mma_shared_inputs[False-ctas_per_cga0-1-1-1-64-0-32-warps2-8-False-True-acc_dtype0]`

Key nearby controls that are also green:
- `python/test/gluon/test_core.py::test_block_m_64_mma[linear]`
- `python/test/gluon/test_core.py::test_tmem_linear_roundtrip_splitn_shapes[linear_m64_splitn_64x32-layout11-64-32-expected_offset_imms11]`
- the 615-case row/col runtime slice spanning:
  - `test_tmem_runtime_matrix_splitn_auto_selects_16x32bx2`
  - `test_tmem_runtime_matrix_splitn_rowcol_permuted_layout_sweep`
  - `test_tmem_runtime_matrix_ldst_rowcol_permuted_layout_sweep`
  - `test_tmem_runtime_matrix_ldst_descriptor_compositions_rowcol_permuted_layout_sweep`

## Root Cause
- The bug was not another packet-offset problem.
- For rank-2 `M=64`, `f32`, 4-warp root loads, the handle-aware TMEM memdesc path could satisfy a split-N request with a speculative `I32x32b` candidate before the correct `I16x32bx2` family was considered.
- Two things caused that:
  - `python/src/gluon_ir.cc` treated `I32x32b` and `I16x32bx2` as interchangeable in `matchesDesiredAtom(...)` for rank-2 `M=64` `f32` descriptors.
  - the handle-aware candidate order prefixed row-plan-specific direct layouts ahead of the generic `getTmemCompatibleLayouts(queryTy, numWarps)` order.
- The public descriptor API already knew the correct split-N family for the same root descriptor, so this was a selection bug, not an ISA limitation.

## Structural Fix
- `python/src/gluon_ir.cc`
  - restore strict atom matching for `I32x32b` vs `I16x32bx2`;
  - align handle-aware candidate ranking with `getTmemCompatibleLayouts(queryTy, numWarps)` before appending memdesc-specific rescue layouts; and
  - try the canonical `M=64` split-N TMEM-linear type first in the public descriptor API for the relevant 4-warp `f32` cases.
- `lib/Dialect/TritonNvidiaGPU/IR/Dialect.cpp`
  - factor a reusable canonical `M=64` split-N linear layout builder; and
  - inject that canonical split-N layout into direct-layout construction and compatible-layout enumeration.
- `third_party/nvidia/lib/TritonNVIDIAGPUToLLVM/TensorMemoryToLLVM.cpp`
  - keep query-type lowering ahead of raw-query for the same `M=64` root-load family so LLVM follows the same structural choice as Gluon.
- `lib/Dialect/TritonNvidiaGPU/IR/TensorMemoryUtils.cpp`
  - keep the earlier validated row-anchor/direct-view fixes that were already supporting the broader row/col runtime frontier.

## Working Principle
- Treat TMEM reinterpret lowering as an exact linear-layout problem, not as a growing list of view-specific rescues.
- The intended durable shape is:
  - derive reinterpret support layouts, origin remaps, row anchors, packet offsets, and atom-family choice from `LinearLayout` compose/invert/pseudoinvert arithmetic;
  - assume there is a sane algebraic lowering for reinterpret/view composition and keep pushing toward it, because once the arithmetic is right the implementation should stop generating whole classes of bugs;
  - expect the arithmetic to be subtle, but treat that as a reason to solve the math cleanly instead of layering more one-off rewrites;
  - delete ad-hoc row/col fixups once the algebraic path exists; and
  - keep clean negatives only where the ISA truly cannot realize the resulting image.

## Linear-Layout Learnings
- The live `block_m_64` reinterpret bug narrowed to a mismatch between two different notions of "packing":
  - memdesc support-query reinterpret was already using exact row-major bit remapping and produced the correct widened sparse support image; but
  - the register side was still being "packed" by hand by deleting a convenient register-col bit.
- That manual register packing is the wrong abstraction boundary. It can preserve enough shape to look plausible while still leaving the sparse-support bit on the wrong input family, which then steers atom selection toward `16x32bx2` instead of the intended packed `32x32b.unpack` path.
- The durable fix direction is:
  - reinterpret the register layout itself with the same exact bit-level row-major arithmetic used for TMEM memdesc reinterpret;
  - compose that reinterpreted register layout with the reinterpreted sparse-support TMEM layout; and
  - only after that derive the direct message family and packet offsets.
- Practical debugging lesson:
  - if a TMEM quotient still carries an unexpected lane/warp basis after a reinterpret, first suspect that one side of the compose was transformed with ad-hoc layout surgery instead of the same reinterpret algebra as the other side.

## Current Packed-Support Debug State
- No new commit yet in this sub-session. The current work is still dirty and centered in:
  - `lib/Dialect/TritonNvidiaGPU/IR/TensorMemoryUtils.cpp`
  - `tmp_blockm64_probe.py`
  - this handoff file
- Closest compiler-only validation remains green after each step:
  - `make -j8`
  - `lit -v test --filter 'TritonNvidiaGPU/(tmem_layouts|ops|invalid|canonicalize)\.mlir'`
- The exact failing runtime nodeids are still:
  - `python/test/gluon/test_core.py::test_tmem_subslice_block_m_64_parent_layout[linear]`
  - `python/test/gluon/test_core.py::test_tmem_subslice_block_m_64[linear]`

## What Changed In This Debug Pass
- Replaced the manual packed-register hack with an algebraic register reinterpret:
  - reinterpret the f16 register layout to a packed-32b `64x64` register layout using the same row-major bit mapping as TMEM memdesc reinterpret.
- Allowed the packed sparse-support path to admit the `M=64` row-zero-lifted `16x32bx2` family instead of rejecting it on principle.
- Moved the packed path into packed-register space by stripping the explicit zero register basis after the algebraic reinterpret.
- Tried restoring the split-N warp anchors (`16,32`) and doubling the half-offset in the packed `M=64` path to match the validated non-packed postprocessing.
- Tried projecting the widened support region back down to the packed logical-view width and explicitly dropping the extra support-band basis from the packed mem layout.

## What These Experiments Proved
- The packed sparse-support path is now definitely the active path for the failing repros.
- The old failure (`2048/8192`, then `2240/8192` with the full kernel) was the broken fallback direct-support lowering, not the packed path.
- Once the packed `M=64` `16x32bx2` path was admitted:
  - first it crashed in LLVM lowering because `perm` still lived in logical 16-bit register space while the generic subword path had already packed values to 32-bit registers;
  - stripping the zero register basis fixed that compiler crash and made the packed path execute.
- After that, the remaining bug moved entirely into packet placement:
  - one intermediate state zeroed only alternating 16-row bands;
  - after restoring split-N warp anchors, the packed path zeroed the entire parent tile;
  - after projecting the packed mem layout to the logical-view width, the behavior still reflected a bad packet decomposition rather than the old fallback path.

## Latest Concrete Observation
- Current debug dump for the parent control shows:
  - packed reg layout out dims `64x64`
  - packed mem layout out dims `64x64`
  - packed quotient out dims `row=128, col=64`
  - packed quotient bases:
    - `register -> col {1,2,4,8,32}`
    - `lane -> row {1,2,4,8} and col {16}`
    - `warp -> row {32,64}`
- The exact runtime repro still fails, and the current observable symptom is that the packed path still decomposes the `M=64` reinterpret into the wrong packet family/packet placement rather than recovering the intended parent columns.

## Most Likely Remaining Core Issue
- The extra support-band / split-N bit is still being assigned to the wrong part of the decomposition.
- More specifically:
  - the packed quotient still carries `register=16 -> col 32` and `lane=16 -> col 16`;
  - that combination is enough to cover the whole `64x128` parent tile when combined with the current `packetOffsets` and `secondHalfOffset`;
  - but the correct reinterpret should only materialize the parent columns corresponding to the half-width `64x64xf32` source slice (`0:32` and `64:96` in the parent test).
- The next fix should not be another offset tweak. It should derive the packed message decomposition from the actual packed logical-view quotient, including which quotient bit is:
  - part of the message tile,
  - part of the packet repetition space, and
  - part of the support-band / subview selection.

## Recommendation For The Next Context Window
1. Keep the algebraic register reinterpret work; it established the right direction and exposed the real remaining bug.
2. Stop trying to recover correctness only by editing `warpBaseOffset`, `secondHalfOffset`, or packet start indices in isolation.
3. Add a small debug helper or one-off probe that prints the final packed `TMemLdStEncodingInfo` for the parent repro:
   - atom
   - `reps`
   - `perm`
   - `numRegsPerMessage`
   - `secondHalfOffset`
   - `warpBaseOffset{0,1}`
   - `packetOffsets`
4. From that, derive a principled decomposition rule for the packed `M=64` reinterpret quotient:
   - identify which quotient bit corresponds to the support-band/subview-selection bit;
   - project or freeze that bit according to the query/view, rather than letting it survive as an ordinary register/packet bit; and
   - only then choose the final atom family.

## Concrete Evidence
- Before the fix, the actual TTGIR root load used a generic linear result layout and PTX scalarized into thirty-two `tcgen05.ld.sync.aligned.32x32b.x1.b32` messages.
- After the fix, the TTGIR root load uses the split-N family:
  - `register [[0,1],[0,2],[0,4],[0,16]]`
  - `lane [[1,0],[2,0],[4,0],[8,0],[0,8]]`
  - `warp [[16,0],[32,0]]`
- PTX now uses `tcgen05.ld.sync.aligned.16x32bx2.x8.b32` for that root load.

## 2026-04-10 Merge-Base Audit: hack inventory, remaining reinterpret buckets, and cleanup order

### Scope
- I audited the compiler-side diff against merge-base `7f61ac734edc657b737fb159a1b9d50cb47944e6` (`master`), excluding `.codex`.
- The overall diff is large and much of it is real feature work, but the TMEM debt clusters into a small number of recurring patch families. Those are the ones to keep checking off rather than continuing to accumulate one-off M64 rescues.

### Inventory Of Patchwork / Hack Families

1. Duplicated TMEM ld/st planning across Gluon, verifier, and LLVM lowering
- Current shape:
  - `python/src/gluon_ir.cc`
  - `lib/Dialect/TritonNvidiaGPU/IR/Ops.cpp`
  - `third_party/nvidia/lib/TritonNVIDIAGPUToLLVM/TensorMemoryToLLVM.cpp`
- Smell:
  - the same raw-query / support-query / row-plan ordering decisions are reimplemented in three layers;
  - several fixes in this initiative only worked after making equivalent changes in multiple places;
  - the previous root-load regression existed because the frontend descriptor planner and LLVM-side lowering stopped agreeing on atom-family choice.
- Desired cleanup:
  - one shared TMEM ld/st planner that returns a structural plan:
    - analyzed support layout
    - row-plan / base contract
    - atom family
    - quotient / repetition
    - packet decomposition
  - Gluon, verifier, and LLVM should validate or consume that plan, not recreate it.

2. M64 family selection is still spread across explicit fast paths
- Current shape:
  - `lib/Dialect/TritonNvidiaGPU/IR/Dialect.cpp`
  - `python/src/gluon_ir.cc`
- Smell:
  - `getCanonicalM64SplitNLayout(...)`
  - `getCanonicalContiguousM64Layout(...)`
  - `getDistributedLayoutForTmemLdStLegacyAnchored(...)`
  - stripped-zero-basis retries
  - exact-family probes for legacy-like layouts
- These were useful to recover broken cases quickly, but they are still family-specific routing logic rather than one physical-family model derived from the normalized linear layout.
- Desired cleanup:
  - canonicalize both TMEM-linear and legacy encodings onto one physical-family representation once;
  - derive family choice from that representation and the quotient, not from a growing menu of M64/M128 special entry points.

3. Reinterpret lowering in `TensorMemoryUtils.cpp` still contains packet surgery
- Current shape:
  - `reinterpretLinearLayout(...)`
  - `reinterpretColumnSubviewSupportQueryLayout(...)`
  - packed sparse-support builders
  - `preferPackedI16x32bx2`
  - `legacyLikeColStride`
  - row-zero-lifted guards and direct-view rewrites
  - explicit `packetOffsets` / `secondHalfOffset` rewrites
- Smell:
  - too many places are manually deleting zero bases, shifting packed col bits, or rewriting anchors after the fact;
  - the current implementation is partly algebraic and partly post-hoc surgery.
- Desired cleanup:
  - define reinterpret lowering entirely as:
    1. transform the memdesc view in exact `LinearLayout` space;
    2. transform the register layout through the same reinterpret algebra;
    3. form the quotient in one physical coordinate frame;
    4. factor quotient bits into:
       - message tile
       - packet repetition
       - support-band / subview-selection
       - row-anchor realization
    5. pick the atom family from that factorization.
- Once that exists, the row-zero/M64 packet fixups should collapse away instead of remaining as durable code paths.

4. Legacy TMEM encodings still have a parallel rescue stack
- Current shape:
  - `getDistributedLayoutForTmemLdStLegacyAnchored(...)`
  - `tryLegacyAnchoredExactFamily(...)`
  - `getLegacyLikeColStride()`
- Smell:
  - legacy encodings are repeatedly pattern-matched back into canonical families inside lowering, instead of being canonically normalized once and then lowered through the same planner as TMEM-linear layouts.
- Desired cleanup:
  - build a single legacy-to-canonical physical view bridge up front;
  - after that, the planner should not care whether the source spelling was legacy or TMEM-linear.
- This is directly tied to the live `legacy` `block_m_64` failure.

5. Row-plan propagation is conceptually right but still too ad hoc
- Current shape:
  - explicit `ttng.tmem_ldst_row_plan`
  - `annotateMMAv5TMemOperandRootRowPlan(...)`
  - many `rowPlanOverride` parameters
  - repeated `getTMemLdStRowPlanForQuery(...)` / `getBackingTMemLdStRowPlan(...)` fallback ladders
- Smell:
  - the initiative proved row plans must be explicit producer-owned contracts, but acquisition and projection of that contract are still scattered.
- Desired cleanup:
  - keep row-plan explicitness;
  - move row-plan acquisition and projection into the shared planner;
  - represent overrides as view-projected contracts, not layer-local rescue logic.

6. Some tests still depend on reinterpret as an implicit physical-layout escape hatch
- Highest-signal examples:
  - `python/test/gluon/test_core.py::test_tmem_subslice_block_m_64`
  - `python/test/gluon/test_core.py::test_tmem_subslice_block_m_64_parent_layout`
- Smell:
  - these tests use `_reinterpret(...)` on TMEM slices to reach specific physical subfragments of TMEM by relying on the compiler's current mapping of logical rows/cols onto TMEM rows/cols;
  - that was historically practical before the descriptor view APIs were generalized, but it is not a strong API contract.
- Desired cleanup:
  - understand each test's user intent;
  - rewrite intent tests to use the explicit descriptor/view APIs (`slice/index/reshape/permute`) that now carry real guarantees;
  - keep only narrow reinterpret smoke tests for what reinterpret itself actually promises.

### Remaining Live Test Buckets, Correlated With PTX

1. `python/test/gluon/test_core.py::test_tmem_subslice_block_m_64[legacy]`
- Intent:
  - take a `64x128xf32` TMEM tile;
  - access the left physical half-band as a widened `64x128xf16` alias;
  - zero just those physical bands; then
  - access three `64x2xf32` physical subfragments and overwrite them.
- Current exact behavior:
  - pytest still fails at `4096 / 8192`;
  - fresh probe shows the wrong bands are exactly `64:96` and `96:128`, while `0:32` and `32:64` are correct.
- PTX correlation:
  - broken legacy path still emits a `tcgen05.st.sync.aligned.16x32bx2.x16.b32` zero-store family for the reinterpret piece;
  - passing linear control emits repeated `tcgen05.st.sync.aligned.16x32bx2.x2.unpack::16b.b32` packets instead.
- Inference:
  - this is not "missing support for the operation";
  - it is a legacy-to-canonical support-family mismatch.
  - The same user-visible operation reaches the right packed-support decomposition for TMEM-linear layout spelling and the wrong raw-query family for legacy spelling.
- Required fix direction:
  - remove the legacy-rescue divergence, not by another packet tweak, but by making legacy memdesc analysis collapse onto the same physical family and quotient as the linear case.

2. `python/test/gluon/test_core.py::test_tmem_subslice_block_m_64_parent_layout[linear]`
- Intent:
  - same physical subview update as above, but written through the parent TMEM register layout instead of asking the subview for its own direct layout.
  - This is the clearest example of user code depending on implicit knowledge of the current TMEM physical mapping.
- Current exact behavior:
  - pytest still fails at `2048 / 8192`;
  - fresh probe shows mismatches only in bands `32:64` and `96:128`.
- PTX correlation:
  - the emitted store is already on the packed family:
    - repeated `tcgen05.st.sync.aligned.16x32bx2.x2.unpack::16b.b32`
  - there is no extra `ttg.convert_layout`.
- Inference:
  - atom-family choice is already good here;
  - the remaining bug is packet decomposition: a quotient bit that represents support-band / subview selection is still surviving as ordinary packet repetition, so the compiler updates the wrong quarter-bands even though the PTX family looks plausible.
- Required fix direction:
  - derive packet decomposition from the quotient itself and explicitly separate support-band bits from repetition bits;
  - do not "fix" this by forcing a different atom family or by reintroducing more offset surgery.

3. The earlier root-load and row/col-permuted bugs are the positive control for this cleanup plan
- Root-load bucket:
  - structural fix changed PTX from thirty-two scalar `tcgen05.ld.sync.aligned.32x32b.x1.b32` loads to the correct `tcgen05.ld.sync.aligned.16x32bx2.x8.b32`.
  - Lesson: when the planner is right, the good PTX family falls out naturally.
- Row/col-permuted runtime-matrix bucket:
  - the initiative already showed that packet/anchor bugs can look like random runtime faults (`misaligned address`) until the quotient/anchor arithmetic is corrected.
  - Lesson: correct linear-layout factoring beats hand-tuned anchor nudges.

### What To Rewrite In Tests

1. Rewrite the `block_m_64` intent tests away from implicit reinterpret mapping
- `test_tmem_subslice_block_m_64`
  - keep the intent:
    - update one physical half-band of the M64 TMEM tile;
    - update three small physical subfragments;
  - rewrite the kernel using explicit descriptor/view composition that names those physical bands through `reshape/permute/slice/index`, then only use `_reinterpret` where the API contract genuinely requires bitcast semantics.

2. Rewrite the parent-layout test into either a guaranteed-view test or a narrow reinterpret smoke test
- `test_tmem_subslice_block_m_64_parent_layout`
  - if the intent is "a user should be able to target this physical subview with guaranteed APIs", rewrite it to those APIs and keep it positive;
  - if the intent is only "the current implicit parent-layout reinterpret happened to work before", do not keep that as a hard semantic contract after changing TMEM lowering.

3. Keep one small reinterpret-specific test surface
- It should check only what reinterpret itself guarantees:
  - bitcount / view preservation
  - legality diagnostics
  - no crashes
  - direct lowering only when the resulting physical support image is actually materializable by the ISA.

### Cleanup Order

1. Unify the TMEM ld/st planner
- Build one shared plan object consumed by Gluon, verifier, and LLVM lowering.

2. Remove the legacy-vs-linear divergence
- Canonicalize legacy physical families once and lower them through the same support/query planner as TMEM-linear layouts.
- This is the next direct code target because it should close `test_tmem_subslice_block_m_64[legacy]`.

3. Replace packet surgery with quotient factorization
- For reinterpret/support queries, derive packet offsets / repetition / support-band selection directly from the quotient in one physical coordinate frame.
- This is the next target after legacy unification and should close the parent-layout bucket cleanly.

4. Rewrite the reinterpret-UB tests to explicit descriptor/view APIs
- Do this once the core logic is stable enough that the rewritten tests can reflect the intended API contract instead of the old compiler accident.

5. Then re-broaden validation
- nearest `block_m_64` tests
- TMEM runtime matrix
- matmul / MMA
- `triton_kernels` matmul
- broader suite

### Checkoff List
- [ ] shared TMEM ld/st planner replaces duplicated frontend/verifier/LLVM planning
- [ ] legacy TMEM encodings canonically lower through the same physical-family planner as TMEM-linear encodings
- [ ] reinterpret packet decomposition is derived from quotient factorization instead of row/offset surgery
- [ ] `test_tmem_subslice_block_m_64[legacy]` green for the same reason as `linear`, not via a special-case rescue
- [ ] parent-layout reinterpret intent rewritten to guaranteed descriptor/view APIs, or demoted to a narrower reinterpret smoke contract
- [ ] broad TMEM + MMA + `triton_kernels` validation rerun after the cleanup

## 2026-04-10: first explicit-view rewrite experiments for the remaining `block_m_64` tests were informative but not yet landable

- I tried to rewrite the two remaining GB200 red tests away from implicit
  `_reinterpret(...)` behavior and onto explicit descriptor/view composition.
- Experiment 1: direct 2D logical slices only
  - replace the borrowed-half-band and `64x2` fragment updates with direct
    `slice(..., dim=1)` subviews on the original `64x128xf32` descriptor
  - result:
    - the kernels compile and run, but they still produce the same wrong output
      buckets as before
    - `test_tmem_subslice_block_m_64[{legacy,linear}]` still lands at
      `4096 / 8192`
    - `test_tmem_subslice_block_m_64_parent_layout[{legacy,linear}]` still
      lands at `2048 / 8192`
  - conclusion:
    - plain logical slices do not restate the old borrowed-physical-fragment
      intent; they preserve the same problematic M64 mapping
- Experiment 2: explicit quarter-band reorder
  - candidate view:
    - `reshape((64, 2, 2, 32)).permute((0, 2, 1, 3)).reshape((64, 4, 32))`
  - rationale:
    - this reorders quarter bands from `[0, 1, 2, 3]` to `[0, 2, 1, 3]`,
      which matches the old `block_m_64` expected borrowed-half-band layout
      much better than plain slices do
  - result:
    - direct stores into the reordered band views fail cleanly during parsing
      with:
      - `unsupported tensor memory descriptor view for direct tcgen05.ld/st`
      - `required row anchors 32,64 are not directly representable in the descriptor view`
    - a broader rank-3 version of the same idea also exposed a
      `getDistributedLayoutForTmemLdSt(...)` assertion on `dims.size() == 2`
      when asking `get_reg_layout()` on the intermediate parent view
  - conclusion:
    - the reordered view is a plausible explicit-contract target, but it is not
      directly lowerable today; the next step is either one more explicit view
      projection or a compiler-side fix for reordered M64 quarter-band views
- Follow-up probe: split rows first, then reorder quarter bands
  - idea:
    - reshape to `2 x 32 x 2 x 2 x 32`, permute the quarter-band dimensions,
      then index each `32`-row half so the final stores only need the already
      supported `32`-row direct frontier
  - throwaway probes:
    - `/tmp/probe_blockm64_reorder_rowsplit.py`
    - `/tmp/probe_blockm64_reorder_rowsplit2.py`
  - result:
    - the looped version hit a Gluon frontend limitation (`'List' object has no
      attribute 'func'`) because Python list iteration in a JIT kernel is not
      supported there
    - the unrolled version got farther but failed at the explicit TMEM view
      level:
      - `failed to infer memdesc_index result type`
      - `unsupported tensor memory memdesc_subslice view; preserved tensor memory view encoding also failed: rank must be less than or equal to the memdesc rank for tensor memory`
  - conclusion:
    - even if the row-split idea is algebraically right, the current explicit
      view path also appears blocked by higher-rank TMEM view/index inference,
      not only by the final ld/st planner
- Durable takeaway:
  - the failed rewrites are no longer just "tests I haven't finished rewriting"
  - they now identify concrete missing TMEM feature/surface-coverage buckets:
    - reordered M64 quarter-band explicit views are not directly lowerable
      today because of row-anchor materialization gaps; and
    - the higher-rank row-split workaround is not yet fully representable by
      current TMEM reshape/index/subslice inference
- Future sessions should remember this when returning to the bug/hack backlog:
  - the right next step is not to preserve old reinterpret behavior by force;
    it is to expand the explicit TMEM view/backend surface until the intended
    contract is actually expressible.
- Important current status:
  - I reverted the experimental test edits after capturing the results above, so
    the worktree returned to the clean committed baseline before the next step.

## 2026-04-10 later checkpoint: warp-specialize partition captures were dropping the explicit TMEM row-plan contract

### Branch / HEAD / Dirty State
- Branch:
  - `codex/tmem-isolated`
- HEAD at the start of this checkpoint:
  - `15647626f`
- Dirty files before checkpointing:
  - `lib/Dialect/TritonNvidiaGPU/IR/TensorMemoryUtils.cpp`
  - `memory.md`
  - `log.md`
  - `handoff_2026-04-09.md`
  - `gb200_branch_recovery_plan.md`
  - `gb200_nvidia_ci_inventory.md`

### Root Cause
- The remaining higher-rank MMAv5-root runtime bucket in
  `python/test/unit/language/test_warp_specialization.py` was narrower than
  the earlier root-annotation hypothesis.
- TTGIR already carried explicit `ttng.tmem_ldst_row_plan` attributes on the
  original `ttng.tmem_alloc` roots.
- The real bug was that `getExplicitTMemLdStRowPlan(...)` only recognized
  direct defining `TMEMAllocOp`s. When a TMEM memdesc crossed a
  `ttg.warp_specialize` partition boundary, the captured block argument lost
  access to that producer-owned contract.
- Inside those partition regions, raw-query planning on
  `ttg.memdesc_index<64x64xf32>` views alternated between:
  - good:
    - `rawRowPlan=128`
    - `rawQuery -> ok atom=4`
  - bad:
    - `rawRowPlan=64`
    - `rawQuery -> ok atom=0`
- That degraded the `tl.dot(p, v, acc)` path at
  `python/test/unit/language/test_warp_specialization.py:491` from packed
  `16x32bx2.x32` MMAv5 loads/stores to scalar `32x32b.x1`, which is what
  triggered the runtime `misaligned address` failure on Blackwell.

### Code Change
- File changed:
  - `lib/Dialect/TritonNvidiaGPU/IR/TensorMemoryUtils.cpp`
- Structural fix:
  - added a helper that maps `gpu::WarpSpecializePartitionsOp` block arguments
    back to the corresponding `getExplicitCaptures()` operand;
  - taught `getExplicitTMemLdStRowPlan(...)` to follow that forwarding edge
    until it finds the original `TMEMAllocOp` row-plan attribute; and
  - kept the change scoped to explicit row-plan recovery instead of adding a
    new warp-specialization-specific planner branch.

### Validation
- Build:
  - `make -j8`
    - green
- Compile-only debug:
  - command:
    - `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0 TRITON_CACHE_DIR=/tmp/triton-cache-warpspec-debug TRITON_DEBUG_TMEM_QUERY=1 TRITON_TRACE_TMEM_QUERY_LOWERING_FILE=1 PYTHONPATH=python:. python - <<'PY' ...`
  - artifacts:
    - `/tmp/ws_true_debug_query.stderr`
    - `/tmp/tmem_query_lowering_trace.log`
  - result:
    - sampled `ttg.memdesc_index<64x64xf32>` raw queries now consistently show
      `rawRowPlan=128`
    - the old `rawRowPlan=64` / `atom=0` branch is gone
- PTX/TTGIR dump:
  - command:
    - `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0 TRITON_CACHE_DIR=/tmp/triton-cache-warpspec-ptx PYTHONPATH=python:. python - <<'PY' ...`
  - artifacts:
    - `/tmp/ws_true_64x64.ttgir`
    - `/tmp/ws_true_64x64.ptx`
  - result:
    - at `.loc 1 491 15`, the old scalar
      `tcgen05.ld/st.sync.aligned.32x32b.x1.b32` sequence is gone
    - packed `tcgen05.ld/st.sync.aligned.16x32bx2.x32.b32` is restored
- Focused runtime exacts:
  - command family:
    - `CUDA_LAUNCH_BLOCKING=1 CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0 TRITON_CACHE_DIR=/tmp/triton-cache-warpspec-... PYTHONPATH=python:. pytest -s --tb=short -vv ...`
  - passing exacts:
    - `python/test/unit/language/test_warp_specialization.py::test_warp_specialize_attention_forward[False-4-False-2-64-64-1024-1024]`
    - `python/test/unit/language/test_warp_specialization.py::test_warp_specialize_attention_forward[False-4-True-3-128-64-1024-1024]`
    - `python/test/unit/language/test_warp_specialization.py::test_warp_specialize_attention_forward[False-8-False-3-128-64-8192-8192]`
    - `python/test/unit/language/test_warp_specialization.py::test_warp_specialize_attention_forward[False-4-True-2-64-64-8192-8192]`
    - `python/test/unit/language/test_warp_specialization.py::test_warp_specialize_attention_forward[True-4-False-2-128-64-8192-8192]`

### Current Interpretation
- The first five representative real exacts from the earlier interrupted
  `test-unit` log are now green for the same structural reason:
  explicit producer row plans survive partition capture and the raw-query
  planner stays on the packed MMAv5 path.
- This is the right level of fix. It removes a contract-loss bug instead of
  layering another M64 or warp-specialization-specific override on top of the
  planner.
- The earlier `128`-nodeid `test_warp_specialization.py` manifest is now
  likely stale, but that is not yet proven until the file or full unit lane is
  rerun.

### Next Concrete Steps
1. rerun `python/test/unit/language/test_warp_specialization.py`
2. rerun `make NUM_PROCS=24 test-unit` if the file-level warp bucket is clean
   or materially smaller
3. refresh the GB200 manifests and inventory from the new unit result
4. continue to the merge-base-present focused `python/test/gluon/test_core.py`
   bucket only after the current-head old-mainline unit bucket is remeasured
5. keep the async-compile/cache investigation separate unless a fresh serial
   process reproduces it without preceding bad kernels

## 2026-04-10 later checkpoint: narrowing the 16-bit unpacked `I32x32b` recursion restores the full `test_mma_shared_inputs` surface

### Branch / HEAD / Dirty State

### Root Cause / Implementation
- I reduced the representative merge-base-pass / current-head-fail
  `test_mma_shared_inputs[...]` exacts with real TTGIR/PTX captures from the
  test harness itself, not a reconstructed kernel:
  - `/tmp/capture_mma_shared_inputs_case.py`
  - current-vs-merge-base artifacts for the representative `g1b` and `g2a`
    exacts now show the real drift point cleanly.
- The regression was not another row-plan propagation issue.
- The current branch had widened the 16-bit unpacked reinterpret rescue inside
  `getDistributedLayoutForTmemLdSt(...)` so far that ordinary direct MMAv5
  accumulator loads were recursing into the generic `I32x32b` builder.
- That changed the physical TTGIR layout and PTX family for representative
  old-mainline exacts:
  - merge-base direct path:
    - packed `32x32b.x8.pack::16b`
  - current broken path:
    - repeated `16x32bx2.x1.pack::16b`
- The fix in `lib/Dialect/TritonNvidiaGPU/IR/Dialect.cpp` is deliberately
  narrow:
  - keep the 16-bit unpacked recursion only for the actual reinterpret/query
    path:
    - `bitwidth == 16`
    - `atom == TMemAccessAtom::I32x32b`
    - `!allowSplitNFastPath`
  - leave ordinary direct loads on the standard bitwidth-packing path so they
    preserve the legacy-equivalent packed row/warp layout.

    exacts now matches the merge-base captures except for file/loc noise.

## 2026-04-10 19:46 UTC: `02-convolution.py` is fixed; live old-mainline queue is now `03-matmul-multicta.py`

### Branch / HEAD / Dirty State
- Branch:
  - `codex/tmem-isolated`
- HEAD at the start of this checkpoint:
  - `0ef8edf23`
- Dirty files before checkpointing:
  - `lib/Dialect/TritonNvidiaGPU/IR/TensorMemoryUtils.cpp`
  - `python/src/gluon_ir.cc`
  - `third_party/nvidia/lib/TritonNVIDIAGPUToLLVM/Allocation.cpp`
  - refreshed initiative docs/manifests:
    - `memory.md`
    - `gb200_nvidia_ci_inventory.md`
    - `gb200_failure_manifest.md`
    - `gb200_branch_recovery_plan.md`
    - `log.md`
    - `gb200_current_branch_examples_convolution_failures.txt`

### Root Cause / Implementation
- I reran the confirmed branch-only `02-convolution.py` OOR on the current head
  and merge-base, then reduced it through current-vs-merge-base TTGIR / LLIR /
  PTX captures of the representative exact
  `python/examples/gluon/02-convolution.py::test_op[0-1-3-3-384-384-64-64-1]`.
- There were three branch deltas in play:
  1. outer-dimension `memdesc_index` on the accumulator view had drifted from
     exact `#tmem` to generic `#tmem_linear`
  2. the Gluon builder had started forcing `tcgen05.mma` /
     `tcgen05.mma_scaled` to `is_async=true` even with no barriers
  3. NVIDIA shared-memory allocation analysis had grown a pessimistic
     `LinearEncodingAttr -> full tile scratch` shortcut for
     `ttg.convert_layout`
- I fixed (1) by preserving exact TMEM encodings across outer-dimension index
  views when the leaf memdesc type is still valid.
- I fixed (2) by restoring the builder-default barrier-driven async selection
  while keeping the MMAv5 row-plan annotation hooks.
- Those two changes cleaned up real abstraction drift, but they did **not**
  clear the OOR. After both were in place, TTGIR was already identical to
  merge-base and the kernel still requested `262208` shared bytes.
- The actual remaining bug was (3):
  - `third_party/nvidia/lib/TritonNVIDIAGPUToLLVM/Allocation.cpp` was treating
    any `ttg.convert_layout` that touched `LinearEncodingAttr` as needing the
    entire source tile in scratch shared memory
  - for the convolution epilogue
    `tensor<256x256xf16, #linear> -> tensor<256x256xf16, #blocked>`, that
    over-allocated an extra `128 KB` scratch buffer and pushed the kernel over
    the GB200/GB300 limit
  - removing that shortcut and leaving the normalized layout-composition
    fallback in place drops the kernel back to merge-base-class shared usage

### Validation
- `make -j8`
- representative compile artifact:
  - current post-fix shared memory:
    - `147520`
  - merge-base reference:
    - `147516`
  - the `+4` byte delta is expected:
    - `kTensorMemoryAllocSharedBytes` for `tcgen05.alloc`
- representative exact:
  - `python/examples/gluon/02-convolution.py::test_op[0-1-3-3-384-384-64-64-1]`
  - `PASSED`
- full file:
  - `python/examples/gluon/02-convolution.py`
  - `48 passed in 7.96s`
- nearby guards:
  - `python/test/gluon/test_core.py::test_mma_shared_inputs[False-ctas_per_cga0-1-1-1-64-0-0-warps0-16-False-True-acc_dtype3]`
  - `python/test/gluon/test_core.py::test_tmem_descriptor_chain_matrix[linear_m64_32x32b_4w-layout0-64-128-32x32b-4-16x32bx2]`
  - both `PASSED`

## 2026-04-10 late multicta reduction checkpoint: root cause is now in the TMEM column-slice raw/support query planner, not in MMA semantics

### Branch / HEAD / Worktree
- branch:
  - `codex/tmem-isolated`
- `HEAD`:
  - `355c2aa20`
- current dirty files:

  - `lib/Dialect/TritonNvidiaGPU/IR/Dialect.cpp`
  - `lib/Dialect/TritonNvidiaGPU/IR/Ops.cpp`
  - `lib/Dialect/TritonNvidiaGPU/IR/TensorMemoryUtils.cpp`
  - `python/src/gluon_ir.cc`
  - `python/triton/experimental/gluon/language/nvidia/blackwell/__init__.py`
  - `third_party/nvidia/lib/TritonNVIDIAGPUToLLVM/TensorMemoryToLLVM.cpp`
- current `apply_patch` runtime backup:
  - `/root/.codex/tooling_backups/apply_patch_runtime_20260410T210943Z`
  - symlink:
    - `/root/.codex/tooling_backups/apply_patch_runtime_latest`

### Exact Repro Still Failing
- command:
  - `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0 TRITON_CACHE_DIR=/tmp/triton-cache-multicta-colslice1 PYTHONPATH=python:. pytest -s --tb=short -vv 'python/examples/gluon/03-matmul-multicta.py::test_matmul_matches_torch[100-200-200-4-32-2-2-CGA_LAYOUT0-8-0-64-128-64]'`
- current result:
  - `9600 / 20000` mismatches (`48.0%`)
- merge-base reference:
  - single packed load family
    - `tcgen05.ld.sync.aligned.16x32bx2.x16.b32`
  - TTGIR reg layout:
    - `register=[[0,1],[0,2],[0,4],[0,8]]`
    - `lane=[[1,0],[2,0],[4,0],[8,0],[0,16]]`
    - `warp=[[16,0],[32,0]]`
- current-head fallback result:
  - split packed family:
    - two `tcgen05.ld.sync.aligned.16x32bx2.x8.b32`
  - TTGIR reg layout:
    - `register=[[0,1],[0,2],[0,4],[0,16]]`
    - `lane=[[1,0],[2,0],[4,0],[8,0],[0,8]]`
    - `warp=[[16,0],[32,0]]`

### Important New Diagnosis
- The failing example uses real TMEM view APIs:
  - `acc_buf.slice(SPLIT_TILE_N * s, SPLIT_TILE_N)`
  - so this is a real compiler/view-planning bug, not a reinterpret-contract test.
- The live bug is no longer “generic MMAv5 wrong-code”:
  - it is specifically the TMEM `64x128 -> 64x32` column-slice direct ld/st
    planner for accumulator epilogues.
- Support/raw direct planning currently fails, so both Gluon and LLVM fall back
  to the standalone query-type path:
  - frontend trace:
    - support-query direct atoms: all `no-layout`
    - raw-query direct atoms: all `no-layout`
    - then `findDirectLayoutForMemDesc queryTy layouts`
  - lowering trace:
    - `supportQuery fail details=`
    - `rawQuery fail details=`
    - `queryType 64x32 ok atom=4 regsPerMsg=8`
- That fallback is exactly why the wrong `x8 + x8` PTX is emitted.

### Core Structural Finding
- The source TMEM slice projection is still built from a non-injective source
  query image:
  - debug source layout for the parent `64x128` slice root:
    - `row=1 -> (1,0)`
    - `row=2 -> (2,0)`
    - `row=4 -> (4,0)`
    - `row=8 -> (8,0)`
    - `row=16 -> (0,0)`
    - `row=32 -> (16,0)`
    - `row=64 -> (32,0)`
  - `computeLeftInverseLayout(...)` on that raw source view fails
  - current error:
    - `unsupported tensor memory memdesc_subslice view`
- The support query that survives today is already collapsed too far:
  - traced column-slice support layout:
    - row bases only `{1,2,4,8,16}`
    - col bases `{1,2,4,8,16}`
    - out dims `64x32`
  - this has lost the row-zero / upper-half source structure that the direct
    planner needs to realize the packed `x16` family.

### What Was Tried In This Slice
- Projected-row-plan wiring cleanup:
  - added `getTMemLdStRowPlanForQueryLayout(...)`
  - rethreaded verifier / Gluon / LLVM to use it
- Exact-view reg-layout probing:
  - tried exact `LinearLayout` planning for explicit `64xN` views
  - tried bypassing the split-N fast path on the direct exact-view builders
- Raw query source-projection repair:
  - when the raw source query is non-injective, retry the origin solve on the
    normalized active source layout instead of bailing immediately
- Query-type fallback row-plan carry:
  - tried preserving the projected `64`-row plan into the standalone query-type
    fallback instead of reverting to the root `128`-row plan
- Pure column-subview shortcut rewrite:
  - rewrote the disabled `inferTMemSubsliceQueryLayout(...)` pure-column rule
    to keep the source row support and only narrow the column dimension
- None of the above is sufficient yet:
  - the repro remains unchanged at `48.0%` mismatches
  - support/raw direct planning still returns `no-layout`
  - the fallback still chooses the bad packed split-N `x8` family

### Current Best Reading
- The next real fix should target the core TMEM column-slice query/support
  construction, not further frontend atom-order surgery.
- Most likely missing behavior:
  - preserve the parent view’s row-zero / upper-half structure through column
    subview raw/support query construction instead of collapsing the slice to a
    partially-active `64x32` analysis layout before direct planning.
- In other words:
  - for pure TMEM column subviews, direct ld/st planning needs the exact
    composed physical query image, not the currently collapsed support image and
    not the standalone query-type fallback.

### Next Concrete Steps
1. instrument or directly inspect the raw-query layout that now reaches
   `inferStandaloneTMemLdStQueryLayoutImpl(...)` after the normalized-source
   inverse retry, and verify whether it still collapses to the same partial
   `64x32` support image
2. fix `inferStandaloneTMemLdStQueryLayoutImpl(...)` /
   `getColumnSubviewTMemLdStSupportQueryPlan(...)` so a pure `64x128 -> 64x32`
   column slice preserves the exact parent row-zero structure
3. rerun the exact multicta repro and require one of:
   - support/raw direct planning succeeds; or
   - TTGIR reg layout returns to merge-base-class
     `register=[[0,1],[0,2],[0,4],[0,8]]`, `lane ... [0,16]`
4. once the exact repro is green, rerun:
   - full `python/examples/gluon/03-matmul-multicta.py`
   - then refresh `gb200_current_branch_examples_multicta_failures.txt`
   - then rerun the full `python/examples/gluon/` directory

    reuses the source support/raw query family and subtracts the already
    materialized subview base offset instead of degrading immediately to the
    sliced `64x32` query type;
  - the TMEM direct planner now rejects `I32x32b` layouts whose warp anchors
    are not aligned to whole `32x32b` message tiles; and
  - the handle-aware explicit split-N frontend path once again preserves the
    planner-returned final split-N layout instead of re-finalizing it.
- Live remaining core issue:
  - `python/test/gluon/test_core.py::test_block_m_64_mma[linear]`
  - current symptom:
    - PTX immediates have drifted to all `32`s, and bypassing that assertion
      exposes real wrong-code rather than a stale expectation
  - most important diagnosis:
    - this is not just another direct-ld/st packet-selection bug;
    - after an explicit split-N TMEM store, later TMEM slices / MMAv5
      consumers still recover addresses from the logical memdesc type alone;
    - the compiler is missing a producer-owned physical TMEM family contract,
      or an equivalent value-aware MMAv5/view-offset model, so it cannot
      preserve the actual physical family chosen by the store through
      slice-after-store uses.
- Next concrete steps:
  1. inspect the exact TTGIR/PTX for `test_block_m_64_mma[linear]` and confirm
     the type-only view-offset/MMAv5 path is still the decisive mismatch
  2. design the narrowest clean producer-visible physical-family contract
     that can survive through TMEM view chains
  3. implement that contract in the TMEM view-offset / MMAv5 path
  4. rebuild and rerun:
     - `test_block_m_64_mma[linear]`
     - the multicta exact + full file
     - the split-N TMEM controls above

### Most Important New Diagnosis In This Slice
- The narrowest likely core bug is now in:
  - `lib/Dialect/TritonNvidiaGPU/IR/TensorMemoryUtils.cpp`
  - function:
    - `getTMemLdStRowPlanForQueryLayout(Value memDesc, MemDescType queryTy, const TMemLdStQueryLayout &queryLayout)`
- Current problematic logic there:
  - it computes `queryPlan`, `layoutPlan`, and `backingPlan`
  - then it has this short-circuit:
    - if the memdesc carries an explicit physical layout and that explicit layout exactly matches `queryLayout.layout`, it returns `backingPlan`
- Why this looks wrong now:
  - for `64x32xf32` M64 queries, the direct ld/st family selection needs the projected `layoutPlan` (`16,32,64` class), not the root/backing `32,64,128` contract
  - the current explicit-layout short-circuit forces the root plan even when the query layout itself already says the direct planner should work on the projected `64`-row footprint
  - that matches the current regression surface exactly:
    - merge-base `test_mma_shared_inputs` uses packed `16x32bx2.x16`
    - current-head uses scalar `32x32b.x1`
    - merge-base multicta uses `16x32bx2.x16`
    - current-head multicta uses `16x32bx2.x8` twice
- I was about to make this exact patch before `apply_patch` failed:
  - remove the explicit-layout-equals-query-layout => `backingPlan` short-circuit entirely
  - broaden the existing projected-M64 override from explicit-view-only to all projected M64 queries:
    - if `queryTy.rank == 2`
    - and `queryTy.elementTypeBitWidth == 32`
    - and `queryTy.shape[0] == 64`
    - and `layoutPlan.rowSpan == queryTy.shape[0]`
    - and `layoutPlan.rowSpan < queryPlan.rowSpan`
    - then return `layoutPlan`
    - otherwise return `queryPlan`
- I did **not** apply that patch because `apply_patch` died before the edit could be made.

### What Changed
- Added explicit MMAv5 producer-root markers:
  - accumulator roots:
    - `ttng.tmem_mmav5_accumulator_root`
  - operand roots:
    - `ttng.tmem_mmav5_operand_root`
- Markers are set from the Gluon/MMAv5 frontend paths and copied through the
  TMEM allocation cloning / partitioning helpers touched in this slice.
- Direct root query-layout planning now preserves the wider backing row plan
  only for explicit MMAv5 accumulator/operand root allocs, avoiding broad
  descriptor-chain inheritance.
- `getExplicitTMemLdStRowPlan(...)` is now public and follows forwarding plus
  descriptor-view chains.
- Pure source-column lowering in
  `third_party/nvidia/lib/TritonNVIDIAGPUToLLVM/TensorMemoryToLLVM.cpp`
  now passes the source support row plan through to the encoding planner.
- The only wide-plan override in that source-column path is scoped to f32
  `64x32` column subviews of wider f32 M64 sources with an explicit/source
  producer contract.

### Root Cause And Boundary
- The failing multicta epilogue had a source support image for the full
  `64x128` accumulator tile, but lowering discarded the source row plan and
  recomputed the narrow sliced `64x32` plan.
- That made the epilogue load disagree with the MMAv5 producer contract.
- A broad outer-index fix was tested and rejected because it broke
  descriptor-chain full-tile indexed loads: those must keep the narrow
  non-MMAv5 raw/support contract.

    `_reinterpret` migration invariant

### New Current Branch Regression
- A full examples/Gluon aggregate rerun after the M64 producer-contract commit
  found one remaining failure:
  - `python/examples/gluon/01-attention-forward.py::test_op[False-dtype0-True-128-1024-48-4]`
- Current-branch exact repro:
  - `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0 TRITON_CACHE_DIR=/tmp/triton-cache-attention-forward-exact-220565b20 PYTHONPATH=python:. pytest -s --tb=short -vv 'python/examples/gluon/01-attention-forward.py::test_op[False-dtype0-True-128-1024-48-4]'`
  - `FAILED`
- Merge-base exact repro:
  - worktree: `/root/code/triton-mergebase-ci`
  - merge-base: `11ee1144a737006921231bbd3386c187812c38e1`
  - `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0 TRITON_CACHE_DIR=/tmp/triton-cache-attention-forward-exact-mergebase-11ee1144a PYTHONPATH=python:. pytest -s --tb=short -vv 'python/examples/gluon/01-attention-forward.py::test_op[False-dtype0-True-128-1024-48-4]'`
  - `PASSED`

### Attention Root Cause Classification
- The attention example reuses an f32 TMEM scratch tile as bf16 P storage via
  `_reinterpret`.
- That is not a supported semantic contract when correctness depends on the
  compiler choosing one particular physical packed store family.
- Current support-query lowering sees the explicit reinterpret support image
  and emits unpacked stores (`tcgen05.st...unpack::16b`), while the old fallback
  happened to emit packed `.b32` stores.
- An ad-hoc lowering-side selector experiment made this exact pass, but the user
  correctly classified that as the wrong direction for this project.

### New Invariant To Preserve
- Any production/example/test code that implicitly relies on `_reinterpret`
  behavior based on compiler physical-layout knowledge should be migrated to
  supported APIs with the same intent.
- The project motivation is to use linear layouts and arbitrary supported view
  manipulation to express these cases directly.
- Do not patch lowering locally just to preserve old `_reinterpret` behavior
  unless the remaining issue is proven to be a real compiler bug for a supported
  view API.

### Next Concrete Steps
1. Find the supported Gluon/TMEM descriptor-view API for expressing the
   attention P-storage aliasing without private `_reinterpret` semantics.
2. Migrate the attention case, and audit nearby `_reinterpret` usages in the
   same file for the same unsupported pattern.
3. Rebuild with:
   - `CPLUS_INCLUDE_PATH=/usr/include/c++/13:/usr/include/aarch64-linux-gnu/c++/13 make -j8`
4. Rerun the attention exact and then the examples/Gluon aggregate.
5. Refresh the GB200 manifests and commit/push once the migration is validated.

## 2026-04-11 05:21 UTC

- branch:

- current committed/pushed `HEAD`:

### Durable API-Migration Reminder
- The user re-emphasized that `_reinterpret` dependencies based on compiler
  physical-layout knowledge should migrate to supported APIs.
- This is the original motivation for the project: use linear layouts and
  arbitrary supported descriptor-view manipulation to express the old intent
  directly.
- The intended primitive is a well-defined `subslice/subview -> bitcast`
  descriptor operation:
  - offset to the intended TMEM region;
  - slice/subview it to the desired physical bits;
  - bitcast to the desired dtype, shape, and layout only when the bitcast
    preserves total size and the exact physical memory mapping of the input
    descriptor.
- Do not preserve the old attention behavior by adding a lowering selector that
  happens to pick the pre-generalization packed store family. Lowering fixes are
  for supported API expressions that miscompile.

### Next Concrete Step
- Stop editing the attention kernel until the supported descriptor bitcast
  operation is designed or found. Then migrate the attention P-storage alias to
  that API and validate the exact failure.

### What Changed
- Added `tensor_memory_descriptor.bitcast(dtype, shape, layout=None)`.
- The operation lowers to a marked `ttg.memdesc_reinterpret`:
  - `tmem_physical_bitcast`
- Contract:
  - offset/slice/subview to the intended physical TMEM bits first;
  - bitcast only when total bits are preserved;
  - preserve the exact physical TMEM mapping of the input descriptor while
    changing dtype/shape and optionally supplying an equivalent typed layout.
- Migrated the attention example's unsupported f32 scratch -> bf16 P alias:
  - `_borrow_s_as_p` now uses `slice(...).bitcast(...)`;
  - `_compute_and_store_exp2` now slices each physical scratch partition and
    bitcasts the partition before storing bf16 P values;
  - one-column alpha/epilogue scratch aliases now use `bitcast`.

### Root Cause And Lowering Boundary
- The old attention path used `_reinterpret` and depended on the compiler
  accidentally choosing a packed `.b32` f16/bf16 store family.
- The supported bitcast path exposed two real lowering requirements:
  - ld/st must use the exact physical bitcast query so sub-32-bit values pack
    into the selected 32-bit TMEM words;
  - MMAv5 typed operand address planning must use the explicit result
    descriptor layout for marked physical bitcasts, because the exact packed
    physical query can be non-surjective and therefore cannot be
    `pseudoinvert()`ed directly for typed MMAv5 coordinates.

### Validation

  - `PASSED`

    16-row message anchor;

    aligned M64 split-N register layout instead;
  - backend default-layout selection has the same canonical fallback so
    `convert_layout` does not assert while routing through this family.

  negatives for now:

    currently materialize;

  - offset to the right part of TMEM;

  - bitcast to the desired dtype, shape, and layout only when equal-size and
    physical-mapping equivalent to the input descriptor.

  `CompilationError`; auto variants can reach the direct descriptor parser and
  raise the clean `RuntimeError`:
  - `lifted row-half TMEM views translate the TMEM row origin`
- The tests still require descriptor-view diagnostics and guard against
  PassManager/assertion crashes.
- Reinterpret migration invariant:
  - do not preserve compiler-knowledge `_reinterpret` behavior;
  - offset to the right part of TMEM;
  - slice/subview to the desired physical bits;
  - bitcast to the desired dtype, shape, and layout only when the total bit
    size and exact physical TMEM mapping are preserved;
  - the bitcast may not change the physical TMEM memory the input descriptor
    maps to.

## 2026-04-11 17:03 UTC

### Branch / HEAD / Worktree
- branch:
  - `codex/tmem`
- latest pushed checkpoint before this docs update:
  - `4521dfe1a`
- remote:
  - `origin/codex/tmem`
- source changes:
  - none

### Current Status
- The supported `_reinterpret` migration contract is now recorded explicitly:
  - offset to the correct physical TMEM region;
  - slice/subview to the desired physical bits;
  - bitcast to the desired dtype, shape, and layout only when total bit size
    and exact physical TMEM mapping are preserved.
- The bitcast must not remap the descriptor to different physical TMEM memory.
  It is a size-and-physical-mapping-equivalent view over the existing selected
  physical region.
- Attention remains reverted for now. Its future migration must combine this
  `offset/slice/subview -> bitcast` view contract with the kernel's
  synchronization discipline, because the kernel deliberately reuses part of
  TMEM while that memory is known to be inactive.

- The new kernel initializes the two-CTA accumulator from a register `C` tile,
  uses cga-aware A/B shared-memory layouts, runs
  `tcgen05_mma(..., use_acc=True, multicast=True)`, and checks
  `matmul(A, B) + C` numerics.
- Each case validates exact PTX/LLIR `tcgen05.mma` kind agreement, exact
  multicast commit opcode emission, `two_ctas` TTGIR, and `tensor_memory_linear`
  TTGIR for canonical accumulator layouts.

## 2026-04-11 post-verifier reminder: supported `_reinterpret` replacement contract

- User clarified the exact intended view operation sequence again:
  - offset to the right part of TMEM;
  - slice/subview to the desired physical bit image;
  - then bitcast to the desired dtype, shape, and layout only when the bitcast
    is size-equivalent and physical-mapping-equivalent to the sliced
    descriptor.
- The bitcast is therefore a view/type reinterpretation over an already
  selected physical TMEM image. It must not select a different TMEM region,
  remap physical storage, or encode attention-specific lowering behavior.
- Keep `python/examples/gluon/01-attention-forward.py` reverted for now. A
  later supported migration must preserve the attention kernel's deliberate
  synchronization discipline when it reuses a TMEM region whose previous value
  is known dead.

## 2026-04-11 two-CTA `warpx2::02_13` patched-PTX sweep

### Branch / HEAD / Worktree
- branch:
  - `codex/tmem`
- latest pushed checkpoint before this probe:
  - `5ff9ece67`
- remote:
  - `origin/codex/tmem`

### Current Status
- Added a reproducible direct-PTX experiment for the no-scales two-CTA
  `tcgen05.cp.cta_group::2.warpx2::02_13.64x128b` frontier.
- The harness starts from the known-good public two-CTA `warpx2::01_23` kernel,
  patches PTX to `02_13`, mutates descriptor immediates and destination offsets,
  assembles with Blackwell `ptxas`, and launches through Triton's normal
  cluster-aware CUDA launcher.
- Results:
  - all eight variants assembled, launched, and returned code `0`;
  - no variant left sentinel values or produced NaNs;
  - no variant matched the extended single-CTA `02_13` oracle;
  - opcode-only, source-row-plus-16, single-seed, and two-message variants still
    duplicate source-column pairs;
  - destination-`+4` variants avoid the simple duplicate-pair predicate but copy
    the wrong row/column mix.
- Conclusion: the direct-seed/address-message mutations still do not encode the
  missing 4-byte source-column bit. Keep the public two-CTA `02_13` candidate as
  a clean unsupported descriptor-plan frontier until descriptor synthesis or a
  documented direct PTX contract can prove correct runtime semantics.

### Current Status
- The remaining `ld/st` audit found a real stale negative gap: direct rank-3
  TMEM access hit a C++ `dims.size() == 2` assertion in the ld/st layout
  planner instead of a clean unsupported diagnostic.
- Direct higher-rank `ld/st` remains future work. The current contract is that
  callers must index/slice/reshape higher-rank TMEM descriptors to a rank-2
  view before direct `get_reg_layout`, `load`, reduction `load`, or `store`.
- Current fix:
  - Gluon descriptor API checks rank first and reports a 2D-only diagnostic;
  - C++ generic and legacy-anchored ld/st planners return no layout for non-2D
    output layouts instead of asserting;
  - `test_tmem_runtime_matrix_ldst_direct_higher_rank_access_reports_clean_error`
    pins auto/explicit register-layout queries plus explicit load/store.

## 2026-04-12 Attention Scratch Alias Bitcast Checkpoint

### Branch / HEAD / Worktree
- branch: `codex/tmem`

- source edits:
  - `python/examples/gluon/01-attention-forward.py`
- docs/status edits:
  - `README.md`
  - `memory.md`
  - `log.md`
  - `handoff_2026-04-09.md`

### Current Status
- Attention is no longer merely reverted/deferred for the focused repro. The
  scratch-borrow helpers and exp2 P-storage partitions now use the supported
  physical bitcast API instead of private `_reinterpret`.
- Important detail: helper-only replacement was insufficient. The final green
  form slices the original f32 scratch descriptor to the exact physical columns
  for each exp2 partition before bitcasting each partition to bf16. That is the
  concrete `offset/slice/subview -> bitcast` contract the user asked us to
  preserve.
- `_borrow_s_as_alpha` and `_borrow_s_for_epilogue` intentionally use
  derived-layout bitcasts for their one-column f32 views; the explicit
  `[SPLIT_M, 1]` layout form still led to the invalid-basis failure during the
  intermediate attempt.
- Full `python/examples/gluon` has not yet been rerun after this focused fix,
  so do not mark the examples lane broadly green from this checkpoint alone.

  shape. On this Blackwell Ultra machine it collects 112 cases covering
  fp16/fp8, both benchmark head dimensions, all benchmark sequence lengths,
  causal/non-causal, and `use_tmem_red` false/true.

  is mean or causal prefix mean. This is necessary because default SDPA tried
  to allocate a 32 GiB attention matrix at `N_CTX=16384`, and cuDNN SDPA is not
  available on `sm_103`.

## 2026-04-12 15:20 UTC: layout-only row-plan cleanup closes preserve-set reds

- Starting point for this slice: `a88f55a81` on `codex/tmem`, dirty in
  `TensorMemoryUtils.cpp` and `TensorMemoryToLLVM.cpp`; target remote remains
  `origin/codex/tmem`.
- Root cause after removing the TMEM side-channel attrs:
  - the LLVM lowering still had a hard-coded f32 `64x128` reinterpret raw-query
    override that replaced exact view-chain layout arithmetic with
    `toLinearLayout(memTy)` and forced `{16, 32, 64}` row anchors;
  - same-dtype `memdesc_reinterpret` views could borrow the source support row
    bit order instead of planning from the destination/view layout;
  - pure outer `memdesc_index` views were using the wider M64 type/family row
    plan even when the concrete inferred query layout had no MMAv5 family block
    dimension, so a store through a chained view and a later load through
    `tmem.index(1)` chose different representatives of the zero-row/broadcast
    layout.
- Fix direction:
  - remove the remaining hard-coded reinterpret raw-query override;
  - keep same-dtype non-physical reinterpret support on the destination/view
    layout when it is standalone-query representable;
  - keep borrowed/wider backing row plans only when the query layout can
    materialize both requested row anchors;
  - for pure outer indexes, use the concrete query layout row plan unless that
    query layout itself still carries the MMAv5 family `block` dimension.
- This preserves the project invariant: the descriptor layout/query is the
  source of truth. Zero bases mean broadcast/equivalence; the planner must not
  choose a divergent hidden physical representative based on producer attrs.
- Current validation against rebuilt compiler:
  - `CPLUS_INCLUDE_PATH=/usr/include/c++/13:/usr/include/aarch64-linux-gnu/c++/13 make -j8`: passed;
  - `make test-lit`: `248 passed, 2 unsupported`;
  - `python/test/gluon/test_core.py::test_tmem_descriptor_chain_matrix`: `26 passed`;
  - all `162` exact nodeids from
    `gb200_branch_new_20260412_unit_main_failures.txt`: `162 passed`;
  - `test_tmem_linear_m64_roundtrip_direct_shapes` plus `test_block_m_64_mma`:
    `20 passed`;
  - `test_tmem_physical_bitcast_preserves_subview_mapping[0]` and `[64]`:
    `2 passed`;
  - hygiene: `git diff --check` passed; removed-attr production sweep has no
    production hits, only negative test assertions.
- Status implication:
  - the preserve-set lit, unit, descriptor-chain, direct-M64, block-M-64 MMA,
    and physical-bitcast checks are green at this checkpoint;
  - older manifests that reported the `162` unit exacts or M64 descriptor-chain
    cases as current red are now stale and must not be used for prioritization
    without rerunning them.
- Remaining after this checkpoint:
  - run broader GB200 grouped sweeps when this slice is committed/pushed;
  - keep the legacy default-load opcode-quality issue on the docket if it still
    appears in a wider sweep;
  - continue the longer plan: supported view/bitcast API migration,
    attention rewrite later, `ld.red`, `copy` warpx2, broader MMAv5 family
    coverage, heuristic cleanup, and staged fuzzing/validation.

## 2026-04-12 23:15 UTC: full-shape MMAv5 loader uses family layout again for tile-preserving descriptors

- The CI-shaped `make test-gluon` rerun after the attr cleanup exposed a focused
  current-head regression bucket in `python/test/gluon/test_tmem_runtime_matrix.py`:
  the tile-permuted MMAv5 accumulator/LHS/scaled positives failed, while exact
  reruns confirmed they were real current-head failures rather than xdist-only
  artifacts.
- Root cause: `DotOpMmaV5TmemLoader::build` had started using the exact
  `tensor_memory_linear` analysis layout before the normal full-shape MMAv5
  family query. That is the right coordinate frame for marked physical
  bitcasts, but it is wrong for full-shape tile-preserving MMAv5 descriptors:
  the exact layout determines whole-tile physical order/offsets, while the
  instruction address arithmetic must stay on the derived MMAv5 family tile.
- Fix: split the loader fallback into an exact typed-layout path and a
  full-shape MMAv5-family path. Marked `tmem_physical_bitcast` descriptors keep
  the exact typed result layout; ordinary full-shape MMAv5 accumulators, scaled
  accumulators, and TMEM LHS descriptors use `getMMAv5*LayoutInfo(...).familyLayout`
  before falling back to exact analysis. No provenance attrs or hidden root
  markers were reintroduced.
- Validation after rebuild:
  - `CPLUS_INCLUDE_PATH=/usr/include/c++/13:/usr/include/aarch64-linux-gnu/c++/13 make -j8`: passed;
  - `git diff --check`: passed;
  - focused tile-permuted failure cluster with `--splits 4 --group 1..4`: groups
    `4 passed`, `4 passed`, `4 passed`, `2 passed`;
  - broad runtime-matrix MMA selector `-k 'mma and not cp'` with four GPU shards:
    group 1 `30 passed, 39 skipped`, group 2 `58 passed, 11 skipped`, group 3
    `69 passed`, group 4 `69 passed`;
  - nearby core M64/physical-bitcast selector with four GPU shards: `7 passed`
    total;
  - representative `test_mma_shared_inputs` exacts covering 1-CTA/2-CTA and
    M64/M128 with four GPU shards: `4 passed`;
  - lit lowering checks: `test/Conversion/tritongpu_to_llvm_blackwell.mlir` and
    `test/TritonNvidiaGPU/mma_lowering.mlir` passed.
- Status implication: the older current-head `test-gluon` failure list containing
  the tile-permuted MMAv5 nodeids is now stale for those nodeids. A full
  `test-gluon`/GB200 rerun is still useful as the next wider milestone, but the
  known branch-caused tile-permuted MMAv5 correctness bucket is fixed at focused
  and nearby-broad scope.

### Next Concrete Steps After This Checkpoint
1. Commit and push this MMAv5 loader fix to `origin/codex/tmem`.
2. Run the next wider GB200/checkpoint sweep. Prefer a CI-shaped command or a
   four-GPU pytest-split equivalent that finishes in the expected minute-scale
   window; treat multi-hour pytest runs as possible hangs or methodology bugs.
3. Continue the branch-recovery plan once the wider sweep confirms no new
   current-head failures: stale lit cleanup if any remains, then the recorded
   longer-term TMEM work (`ld.red`, `copy` warpx2, broader MMAv5 family
   coverage, heuristic cleanup, and fuzzing/validation).

## 2026-04-13 14:42 UTC: scales `warpx2` direct-source-offset scan is negative

- Extended `experiments/probe_cp_scales_warpx2_direct_ptx.py` so it can patch
  arbitrary single copy messages: opcode family (`warpx4`, `warpx2::01_23`,
  `warpx2::02_13`), direct source-offset immediate, and TMEM destination delta.
  The parent mode runs one child process per variant and can write JSONL records,
  which keeps illegal-memory variants from poisoning later probes.
- New scan results:
  - `warpx4` source-offset `0`, `dst_delta=0` copies logical columns `0..7`;
  - `warpx4` source-offset `32`, `dst_delta=4` copies logical columns `8..15`
    exactly, matching the second canonical `warpx4` message;
  - `warpx2` source-offset `0`, `dst_delta=4` writes into logical columns
    `8..15`, but duplicates source logical columns `0..7` there;
  - `warpx2` source offsets `0..8` are legal at `dst_delta=4` but only select
    row-shifted low physical columns; no legal offset produced exact logical
    columns `8..15`;
  - `warpx2` source offset `32` faults for both `01_23` and `02_13`, including
    at `dst_delta=0`, so the fault is tied to the source descriptor window, not
    the destination address;
  - additional offsets `64..72` and `120..136` also faulted.
- Interpretation:
  - the current scales shared layout stores logical columns `8..15` in the
    row+32 physical window that a 32-row `warpx4` message can legally address;
  - a 64-row `warpx2` message cannot use that same source offset on the 64-row
    source tile, so simply swapping the canonical second `warpx4` opcode to
    `warpx2` is not a valid support strategy;
  - true scales `warpx2` support would need a different descriptor/view/staging
    model that presents the high logical half as a legal 64-row source window,
    or it should remain clean unsupported.
- Validation/evidence:
  - `python3 -m py_compile .codex/initiatives/tmem_linear_generalization/experiments/probe_cp_scales_warpx2_direct_ptx.py` passed;
  - four-GPU scan outputs were recorded under
    `experiments/results/probe_cp_scales_warpx2_offsets_*_dst4_gpu*.jsonl`;
  - smoke probes reconfirmed the existing `warpx4_control` match and the
    `first_01_23_only` low-half behavior.
- Next:
  - do not add production lowering for scales `warpx2` from these direct-source
    offsets;
  - if returning to this frontier, derive a real shared-memory descriptor/view
    schedule first, likely involving a different parent/subview physical shape;
  - otherwise move to the next long-term bucket (`ld.red` breadth, copy
    `warpx2` non-scales frontier, or broader MMAv5/scaled-MMAv5 saturation).

## 2026-04-14 10:03 UTC: handoff after copy warpx2 conversion-lit / layout-asm checkpoint

- Current source state:
  - `lib/Dialect/TritonGPU/IR/LinearLayoutAsm.cpp` now supports optional explicit output codomain sizes in linear-layout assembly via `out = [...]`, and prints that field for non-surjective layouts.
  - `test/Conversion/tritongpu_to_llvm_blackwell.mlir` now has conversion checks for the already-supported no-scales `warpx2` copy opcodes: single-CTA `01_23`, single-CTA `02_13`, and two-CTA `01_23`.

- Why this was needed: Gluon can construct non-surjective TMEM-linear layouts with a zero row basis for `warpx2` broadcast/equivalence semantics. Without explicit `out = [...]`, textual MLIR round-trips infer the smaller surjective image and lose the logical memdesc shape, making lit tests reject layouts that runtime lowering already supports.
